## Q4 (2026-11-05, after round 1 of the ticket reviews) - decisions that belong to the lead / owner
Round 1 is finished for all four tickets: 8 reviewers, 138 findings, 12 BLOCKERS, all OPEN in `handover/ledger.md` (rows `BL-core-<pkg>-r1-*`; reports in `handover/reviews/`). The `egm:egm-resolve` skill cannot be started by the cloud session (the tool refuses: reserved for the user), so the fix batch has NOT started. None of the tickets is ready to build.
Decisions the reviews need from you before the tickets are rewritten (my recommendation first):
1. **P4d vs F2 (who owns the Postgres money SQL).** F2 puts the Postgres implementation of reserve/settle in P4b1; the P4d draft built it in P4d. Recommendation: keep it in P4d (the contract rows must go green on Postgres) and amend F2 in `amendments.md`; split P4d into three pull requests (A12 of the adversarial review): P4d-a harness and existing-method fixes, P4d-b money methods + migration, P4d-c lock-in guard and test-file changes.
2. **P4d money-method rules** (3 blockers): the cap must be checked on the first insert as well as on conflict (one UPDATE ... WHERE shape, both counters in one transaction); settle must be one atomic claim (`WHERE status='open'`), lock order reservation, day, month; a replay of a settled id must be refused. Also refuse NaN/Infinity/negative amounts in the OLD `add_spend`/`add_cost` now, not in P4b1.
3. **P2a identity (3 blockers):** the allow-list compares a typed name, not an authenticated identity, so an agent that holds the store can clear the stop directly. Recommendation: stop stays open to anyone (audited), reset is restricted, and the real enforcement is a separate database role for reset (runtime role gets read-only on `kill_switch`). Also: check the raw name before masking, mask once at the choke point, and name an owner for wiring `StopGate` into the run path (today nothing calls it).
4. **P1 identity key (3 blockers):** one string for identity; the two readings of Cyrillic Ze live only in the DENY key-set, with a fixed size cap that fails closed; the new errors must not derive from `ValueError`; define the key-set encoding and match rule.
5. **P3a (2 blockers):** the refusal message must give a recipe that works (apply the file by hand and insert the ledger row, not "renumber"); do the symlink refusal only in the CI check, not at runtime in `migrate()`.
6. Smaller ratifications: the P1 "which PR #50 head" (2e8a28c vs 748849c) for every number; the plan's P3a "duplicate number" fail-before is not real (already refused on main); the CHANGELOG lines 10-11, 38, 45, 52 that mention `0006_evidence` need rewording, not only an appended correction.

## Q5 (2026-11-05, after round 2 of the ticket reviews) - what the lead needs to know and decide
Round 2 (8 fresh reviewers, ticket revision 3) is finished. Result: NO ticket is clean. Blockers: P1 1 (correctness: same_person weakened by hidden tag characters), P2a 3 (adversarial 2 + correctness 1), P4d 1 (lock order). P3a has no blocker (9 majors across two reviews). Totals: 5 blockers, 55 majors, 58 minors (118 round 2 rows); ledger rows `BL-core-<pkg>-r2-*`. Round 1 had 12 blockers, so the tickets improve, but each rewrite also exposes problems in the new design.
**Decision needed for P2a (two reviewers independently called the database part unbuildable as written):** (a) the runtime stop is an INSERT ... ON CONFLICT, so a runtime grant of SELECT, UPDATE makes the stop fail; (b) no route exists for a separate reset credential (no DSN setting, no second store, `release` runs inside the runtime process), (c) the trigger cannot learn the reset role name from a migration file, (d) one shared reset role gives no per-person attribution. Route A (recommended by the cloud session): keep the trigger design but specify it fully (grant lists, a reset DSN secret name in the store settings, `migrate(reset_role=...)` creating the trigger function with the role name, `release` as an operator-process action on a reset-role store, per-person login roles in a NOLOGIN reset group, `ENABLE ALWAYS TRIGGER`, start-up probe). Route B: no trigger; stop open to everyone and audited, reset an operator action with its own credential, and say plainly that the code check is a typo guard, not access control. Without your decision the cloud session applies Route A in the next rewrite.
Other lead-level points from round 2: (1) amendment A13 wording says "P4d-c as its adversarial review A12 defines", but A12 defines no such thing: P4d-c is the lock-in guard; please correct. (2) The P1 ticket cited "A13 decision N" for items A13 does not contain; those are the author's proposals and will be marked PENDING LEAD. (3) P1: should the key-set also cover I/l/1/O/0 look-alikes (16 keys) or does DENY for ids use `id_key(loose)` over the whole text? Recommendation: the second (explicit view), plus a substring API `scan_contains`. (4) P1: Intake's ALLOW move needs raw spans; recommendation: P1 does not move the Intake ALLOW checks; Intake keeps `_f` equality until an Intake ticket does. (5) P4d: add a terminal status `expired` and `list_open_reservations` now, while the Protocol is still open (changing a closed method later is not additive).

## Q12 (from the look-alike round 7 resolve; evidence run on the old-code snapshot, no spend)
Order 14(2) says that for secrets of 8 to 11 characters "only plain and re-cased copies are" caught. The resolve ran the old code: upper-case and lower-case copies of such a secret are SENT (as are look-alike, combining-mark and split-line copies); only the plain copy is masked on every path (full-width and zero-width copies are also masked on the `redact_text` paths). The design (draft 8, PL-LK-42) therefore says "only plain copies" and does not claim re-cased copies are caught. Recommended answer: confirm the corrected wording (option a). Alternative: a case-folded literal sweep for secret-kind values of 8 to 11 characters (new surface, false-mask cost; v1.1 row LKV-2). Proceeding with the recommendation under orders 6(g) and 9.

Answer to q295 question 1: __reduce__ CAN go. With __init__(self, message, problems=()) doing self.problems = tuple(problems) then super().__init__(message), BaseException.__reduce__ round-trips with no custom method (pickle, copy, deepcopy checked in a scratch script outside the repo; the repo's own pickle test was NOT run against it). The scan then needs only "a site class defines nothing but __init__", so every pickle method name is refused and the exact-shape __reduce__ check goes away. Costs: signature change on an internal error class (1 src site migration_numbers.py:166, 6 test constructions); an old-style MigrationNumberError(list) would silently build with message=list, so needs a test that fails on it; problems must stay defaulted. My recommendation: take it (simpler scan, fewer hostile cases), but it invalidates the queued 0b54631 runs, so I do NOT apply it until you say yes (or after the 0b54631 results land). Tell me which.
P5b3 note: producers guarded module-wide by hashing producer functions plus reachable defs beside each non-LITERAL row, capped UNGUARDED state, relay detection for str(e) of a caught site class. 41 of 46 mutants are single-edit and port to the P1m runner; 5 (M8c, M14a, M24d, M26, M35) need splitting; not run.

## q (cloud, A3 round 2): rename or caveat two payload fields?
M2: `access.portal_read_only:true` only means the /api/ui read connection is read-only; the portal also has Retry and Mark as sent. Proposal: rename to `ui_reads_read_only` and add `portal_can_change:["retry","resolve"]`. M3: `never_receives` lists what the AI step is not passed, but a live step can look up name/phone/company/message by lead_id. Proposal: add note `can_look_up_with_lead_id`. Both change the frontend contract; need your yes. Report: handover/demo/reports/portal-A3-review-2-security.md

## q (cloud, fuzz evidence ORDER 216): slow-sender hole in server.py, build a patch?
Evidence: handover/demo/reports/fuzz-ui-routes.md. 20 sockets sending 1 byte per second (partial request line or headers) were all still open at 45 s. Cause (read in server.py): `timeout = REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (30 s) is a limit per read, not for the whole request, so each byte restarts it; the reading of the request line and headers has no total deadline (only the drain code has one). One client can hold a handler thread for as long as it likes; threads are not capped. Also: a client that closes before reading an error reply makes `send_error` raise BrokenPipeError, logged as a traceback by the standard library (15 log lines, 5 tracebacks). Proposal: one wall-clock deadline for request line plus headers (e.g. 10 s, in constants) and a cap on live handler threads; catch BrokenPipe in `send_error`. Needs your yes because it changes server.py in every tree. Say "build S1" and I will make patch-portal-S1.diff with fail-before tests. Not tested: real static files (the tree has no portal/ folder), /api/demo, pipelining, slow bodies.

## q-A11a-ascii-marks (PR77 / P2d-1, 08:2xZ)
Question: must ASCII `^` and backtick next to a value on an ALL-ASCII line be masked with the value? Today the hidden-character scan runs only for lines that contain a non-ASCII character, so `AKIA^...` on an all-ASCII line is unmasked (same on 8b2b716). Folding every backtick line would send all markdown through the 2x view scan (cost). DEFAULT: not folded; boundary stated in the CHANGELOG. Say if you want it folded.
Also: I told the PR77 builder to fix control characters (VT/FF/NEL/FS/TAB/ESC inside a key leak it; also on 8b2b716) in PR77 before reviews start (fix-dont-defer). DEFAULT: yes; the merge of PR77 is delayed about 45-60 min. Tell me if you prefer to merge 76adadb first and do the control-character fix as a follow-up.

## q-A11a-sep-keep (PR77, 2026-11-11, redaction)  (2026-11-11 10:50Z: round 6 reviewers reproduced leaks at the 4th run; the builder is replacing the view enumeration, so the 3-versus-2 question is moot unless the replacement fails)
Builder a15af6a6 reports a cost-versus-coverage trade-off in the tab-like control views: only the first 3 runs of tab-like characters on a line are tried as kept separators (`_SEP_KEEP = 3`); a key glued to a word by a 4th or later run and cut by another run is still read with all runs dropped and with all kept, but not with a mixed set. With 3 kept runs a 241,500-character 4-column tab table takes 5.9 to 6.0 s CPU against the 6.0 s limit (head before: 2.2 s); with 2 kept runs 3.3 s. Over the limit the input is refused (fails closed, nothing shown). DEFAULT (unless you say otherwise): keep 3 only if fresh reviewers find no leak at the 4th run and the clock refuses cleanly; if a reviewer shows a leak at the 4th run or the refusal is machine-speed dependent, use 2 kept runs plus the all-dropped and all-kept views and state the boundary in the CHANGELOG.

## 15:34Z UTC Redaction: is a tab inside a key something we must mask? (default taken: no, same as a space)
A tab inside a secret key (for example the first half of a key, a tab, the second half) is treated like a space inside a key: not masked, and stated as a limit. The stricter choice (ignore tabs when matching) made ordinary tab-separated tool output unreadable in round 7. If you want the stricter rule for vendor-prefixed keys only, say so: it is a contained later change. Decision record: D-018.

## 15:53Z UTC Database migrations (P3a-2): what we promise, and two defaults (D-020)
1. Promise, in plain words: the migration runner stops honest mistakes in our own files and detects lasting excess rights; it does NOT stop a deliberately hostile migration file (that file runs as the owner of the tables; no check running as the same database role can). Earlier texts claimed more. If you want hostile files stopped, the control is a separate read-only checker identity and review of files, not more rounds on this runner. Default taken: promise as above.
2. Every PostgreSQL database lets every role create temporary tables by default. Default taken: stated as normal, not refused. Say if you want it refused (existing setups would then fail until an administrator revokes it).
3. A migration role that OWNS the database stays allowed (default taken), and is named as a risk in the hardening guide. Say if it should be refused.

## Q-2026-11-11-Q8a1-direct-store-purge: should a pilot or prod app refuse the evidence purge when it is called straight on the store?
- Fact (independent evidence on head 0b97d55a, probes-a-c.txt): on a pilot or prod app, `store.purge_tool_calls(<time 500 days ahead>, ...)` and `store.purge_redaction_maps(...)` deleted all 3 planted rows with no refusal. The tick refuses a made-up time (Q8a-1), but a caller that reaches the store directly is below that refusal. The same holds for `Approvals.sweep(at=)` and `resend_unsent(at=)`.
- Why not fixed in Q8a-1: the store knows no stage and has no trusted clock; a guard inside it would have to be given a stage (a change to the store contract and to both stores) and demo tests rely on made-up times. That is more than a small choke-point fix.
- Safe default taken: not changed; stated plainly in the CHANGELOG, `build` and `run_tick` docstrings and the ticket errata rev 7 (the refusals stop a test object that arrives by accident, not code that deliberately calls the store).
- Proposed fix if you want it (a separate ticket): the app hands the deleting identity (`purge_store`) to the tick as a small object that holds the stage and the app's clock and refuses a cut-off later than its clock outside demo; the raw store methods stay for demo and tests. Say yes or no.

(a) Rev 2 refuses, before any file runs, a function or type of another application that the migration role owns in the schema of the run and that everyone may use (round 12 let it pass). (b) Rev 2 judges rights of roles OTHER than the three named roles and everyone only inside the schema of the run (option (b) of the design review, an AUTHOR ADDITION that narrows D-020's "objects the migration role family owns"). If the answer is "not supported", widening to every schema costs one changed condition in one query plus tests; it would refuse every setup where the migration role also owns another application's schema. Safe default taken: refuse (a), keep (b) narrow, and say both in the CHANGELOG. Please answer before the build of work package A1 starts, or accept the default.
2. Rule O5 is struck (design review D-M7): other members of a group role that owns the package's tables are not judged and can act as owners; written as a non-promise. Three tests that pinned "a file hands an object to a group the migration role belongs to is refused" are retired with it (ticket 5.4). Please confirm or reject the strike.
3. CI route for `max_prepared_transactions` above 0 (ticket 6.3) is still untried. Default: the prepared-transaction test fails on any server where the setting is 0, so it can never pass without running. The first route the builder tries changes a step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (docker exec + restart of the service container); no repository setting changes.

## 2026-11-11 ~17:20Z UTC (22:50 IST) P3a-2 ticket rev 3 (last plan round): five owner decisions, defaults taken
Plain words first: `migrate` is the program that sets up the package's database and then checks that the three application logins (runtime, portal, purge) can do only what the package's list of rights allows. Each item below has a default already written into ticket rev 3 (section 10.3); a different answer changes only the named rules and tests.
1. OWNER QUESTION, one question with two consequences (ticket 10.3 items 5, 11, 14): may this package share its migration login, or its schema, with another application? Default taken: NO. Consequence A (applied now): a function or type of another application in the package's schema, owned by the migration login and usable by everyone, is refused before any migration file runs. Consequence B (not applied now): outside the package's schema, `migrate` judges only what the three logins and everyone can do, not what other roles can do there. If you answer "not supported", B could be widened to every schema (one changed query clause, its tests; a new ticket revision). If you answer "supported", A is re-reviewed, and the new "NOT THIS PACKAGE'S OBJECT" marking on printed statements becomes the main safety for the administrator.
2. OWNER QUESTION (10.3 item 13): a database extension such as `pg_stat_statements` (a very common performance add-on) installs two views that EVERYONE may read; tested on PostgreSQL 16.15 (evidence handover/evidence/P3a-2/plan-r3/p3b.out I1-I4), and an extension update gives that right back after an administrator removes it. Default taken: such a database is REFUSED, with no removal statement printed, and a line that says the object belongs to the extension. Option b: for objects that belong to an extension we do not own, judge only the three logins, not everyone. Cost of b: one rule, one mutant, two tests.
3. OWNER SIGN-OFF (10.3 items 3 and 15): rule O5 is struck. Other members of a group role that owns the package's tables can act as owners and are not judged (written down as something `migrate` cannot notice). Three earlier refusals are dropped on purpose (ticket 5.4, table "why"). Default: struck.
4. CI route (10.3 item 10, unchanged from rev 2): the prepared-transaction test needs a CI PostgreSQL with `max_prepared_transactions` above 0; the route is untried. Default: the test FAILS where the setting is 0, so it can never pass without running.
5. CI cost of the test matrix (10.3 item 16, about 1,000 generated cases): default taken: one CI job on every pull request, required for merge; if the builder measures more than 20 minutes, a separate required job, never fewer cases and never nightly-only. This repository is public, so GitHub-hosted minutes are normally free; UNVERIFIED for your plan.
